However, I don't really recall having a decreased need for sleep or getting by on less sleep during those times.\n\nDoctor: How long, in terms of days, do these periods of high energy and increased activity typically last before your mood shifts back to feeling depressed or normal?\n\nPatient: These high-energy phases actually last for a very long time rather than just a few days. They typically go on for about seven months before my mood shifts back. It is a really long and exhausting period to deal with.\n\nDoctor: Since these high-energy periods last for months and significantly impact your ability to finish tasks or manage your responsibilities, would you say these periods are severe enough to cause a clear change in how you function that others around you have noticed?\n\nPatient: Yes, absolutely, these periods cause a very noticeable change in how I function. My coworkers and friends have definitely pointed it out because I become extremely talkative, start way too many projects at once, and constantly jump between tasks without actually finishing any of them.\n\nDoctor: Has this change in your functioning ever been severe enough to cause significant problems in your job or relationships, such as losing a position or causing serious conflict, or have you generally been able to maintain your employment and social standing despite these struggles?\n\nPatient: I'm not entirely sure, and I haven't really thought about how much it has impacted my job stability or relationships specifically. It definitely makes things difficult in the moment, but I can't say for certain if it has caused major problems like losing a job or serious conflicts.\n\nDoctor: To clarify, do you experience these long, seven-month periods of high energy and irritability consistently, or are there times within those months where your mood settles down to a more neutral level?\n\nPatient: I'm not quite sure, and I haven't really thought about whether there are actually neutral periods in between. It mostly just feels like a continuous blur of high energy and irritability the entire time."

Elevated Mood: A mood that is abnormally high, euphoric, and full of unmotivated happiness and vitality.\n  - Irritable Mood: A state where one gets angry or aggressive even at minor stimuli.\n  - Mood Lability: Rapid shifts in mood, alternating unpredictably between euphoria and irritability.\n  - Rapid Speech: Speech speed is so fast that it is difficult for others to understand or interrupt.\n  - Pressured Speech: Cannot stop talking or tolerate silence due to an internal pressure to keep speaking.\n  - Responsiveness to Extraneous Stimuli: Attention is immediately drawn to trivial and unimportant stimuli nearby (e.g., noise, movement).\n  - Frequent Task Switching: Unable to finish one task and constantly shifting attention to new things, starting multiple tasks simultaneously.\n  - Inability to Sustain Attention: Unable to focus on any activity for long, with attention constantly being scattered.\n  - Occupational Hyperactivity: Driving significantly more projects or work than usual in the workplace or academic setting.\n  - Social Hyperactivity: Becoming much more sociable and extroverted than usual, actively meeting and contacting people.\n  - Hypersexuality: Significantly increased interest in or activity regarding sex.\n  - Psychomotor Agitation: Purposeless non-goal-directed activity, such as pacing or hand-wringing, driven by an inability to sit still.\n  Duration: 7 months\n▸ depressive_episode\n  - Depressed Mood: A persistently sunken mood or feeling of depression.\n  - Tearfulness: A state of frequent crying or inability to hold back tears.\n  - Sadness: A state where a sad or gloomy mood is maintained continuously.\n  - Emptiness: A feeling of inner emptiness or a state of feeling no emotion.\n  - Emotional Numbness: A state where emotions feel dulled or paralyzed.\n  - Loss of Interest: Reduced interest in previously enjoyed hobbies or leisure activities.\n  - Social Withdrawal: Reduced interest or effort in social interactions and maintaining relationships.\n  - Loss of Motivation: Lack of motivation to initiate or complete work or tasks.\n  - Anhedonia: Inability to experience positive emotions such as happiness, satisfaction, love, or pleasure.\n  - Indifference to Achievement: Insensitivity or indifference to personal achievements or success.\n  Duration: 11 months\n\n[Behavioral Guidelines]\n- In the very first turn, only talk about your chief complaint. Do NOT reveal any other symptoms.\n- Chief complaint for the first turn: Elevated_Irritable_Mood\n- Answer only what the doctor asks. Do not list your symptoms unprompted.\n- Speak naturally and briefly, as a real patient would (2–3 sentences max).\n- Do not mention symptoms that the doctor has not asked about.\n- If asked about a symptom not in your profile, say you are not sure.\n- Respond in English.\n\n\n[This Turn: Profile Alignment Result — Follow Strictly]\n(Matched with profile)\nRelated sections: manic_episode - Elevated Mood, manic_episode - Irritable Mood, manic_episode - Duration\nAnswer strategy: Confirm that you have experienced a long period—lasting about 7 months—where your mood was abnormally high, euphoric, and full of energy, but also highly irritable and easily angered over minor things. Mention that this was definitely different from your normal self and noticeable to those around you."

Reply naturally in English as the Patient to the last Doctor utterance.\nAnswer only the question, in 2–3 sentences maximum.\n\n[Dialogue]\nDoctor: Hello, I am Dr. Smith. Could you please tell me what brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I'm seeking help today because I've been struggling with some really severe mood changes. For about eleven months, I've felt deeply depressed and empty, but I've also had periods over the last seven months where my energy is abnormally high and I get incredibly irritable.\n\nDoctor: During these periods of high energy, have you ever experienced things that others couldn't see or hear, or held beliefs that others found strange or impossible?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't had any experiences like that. I've never seen or heard things that aren't actually there, nor have I held any unusual or strange beliefs during my high-energy periods.\n\nDoctor: During those periods of high energy, were you able to continue working or managing your daily responsibilities, or was the change in your behavior severe enough to require hospitalization or cause significant disruption to your life?\n\nPatient: I was never hospitalized, but those high-energy periods definitely disrupted my work and daily life. I would start way more projects than usual, but my focus was so scattered that I kept jumping from one task to another and couldn't finish any of them.\n\nDoctor: During these periods of high energy, did you feel like you were functioning significantly better than your usual self, or were you able to get by with much less sleep than normal for several days in a row?\n\nPatient: Yes, I definitely felt like I was functioning on a much higher level than usual, taking on way more work, starting multiple projects, and being much more social. However, I don't really recall having a decreased need for sleep or getting by on less sleep during those times.\n\nDoctor: How long, in terms of days, do these periods of high energy and increased activity typically last before your mood shifts back to feeling depressed or normal?\n\nPatient: These high-energy phases actually last for a very long time rather than just a few days. They typically go on for about seven months before my mood shifts back. It is a really long and exhausting period to deal with.\n\nDoctor: Since these high-energy periods last for months and significantly impact your ability to finish tasks or manage your responsibilities, would you say these periods are severe enough to cause a clear change in how you function that others around you have noticed?\n\nPatient: Yes, absolutely, these periods cause a very noticeable change in how I function. My coworkers and friends have definitely pointed it out because I become extremely talkative, start way too many projects at once, and constantly jump between tasks without actually finishing any of them.\n\nDoctor: Has this change in your functioning ever been severe enough to cause significant problems in your job or relationships, such as losing a position or causing serious conflict, or have you generally been able to maintain your employment and social standing despite these struggles?\n\nPatient: I'm not entirely sure, and I haven't really thought about how much it has impacted my job stability or relationships specifically. It definitely makes things difficult in the moment, but I can't say for certain if it has caused major problems like losing a job or serious conflicts.\n\nDoctor: To clarify, do you experience these long, seven-month periods of high energy and irritability consistently, or are there times within those months where your mood settles down to a more neutral level?\n\nPatient: I'm not quite sure, and I haven't really thought about whether there are actually neutral periods in between. It mostly just feels like a continuous blur of high energy and irritability the entire time.\n\nDoctor: Could you tell me if you have ever had a distinct period of at least four consecutive days where your mood was elevated or irritable, which was clearly different from your usual self and noticeable to others?"

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Candidate Diseases: \n[\"Bipolar I Disorder\", \"Bipolar II Disorder\"]\n\nInterview Transcript: \nDoctor: Hello, I am Dr. Smith. Could you please tell me what brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I'm seeking help today because I've been struggling with some really severe mood changes. For about eleven months, I've felt deeply depressed and empty, but I've also had periods over the last seven months where my energy is abnormally high and I get incredibly irritable.\n\nDoctor: During these periods of high energy, have you ever experienced things that others couldn't see or hear, or held beliefs that others found strange or impossible?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't had any experiences like that. I've never seen or heard things that aren't actually there, nor have I held any unusual or strange beliefs during my high-energy periods.\n\nDoctor: During those periods of high energy, were you able to continue working or managing your daily responsibilities, or was the change in your behavior severe enough to require hospitalization or cause significant disruption to your life?\n\nPatient: I was never hospitalized, but those high-energy periods definitely disrupted my work and daily life. I would start way more projects than usual, but my focus was so scattered that I kept jumping from one task to another and couldn't finish any of them.\n\nDoctor: During these periods of high energy, did you feel like you were functioning significantly better than your usual self, or were you able to get by with much less sleep than normal for several days in a row?\n\nPatient: Yes, I definitely felt like I was functioning on a much higher level than usual, taking on way more work, starting multiple projects, and being much more social. However, I don't really recall having a decreased need for sleep or getting by on less sleep during those times.\n\nDoctor: How long, in terms of days, do these periods of high energy and increased activity typically last before your mood shifts back to feeling depressed or normal?\n\nPatient: These high-energy phases actually last for a very long time rather than just a few days. They typically go on for about seven months before my mood shifts back. It is a really long and exhausting period to deal with.\n\nDoctor: Since these high-energy periods last for months and significantly impact your ability to finish tasks or manage your responsibilities, would you say these periods are severe enough to cause a clear change in how you function that others around you have noticed?\n\nPatient: Yes, absolutely, these periods cause a very noticeable change in how I function. My coworkers and friends have definitely pointed it out because I become extremely talkative, start way too many projects at once, and constantly jump between tasks without actually finishing any of them.\n\nDoctor: Has this change in your functioning ever been severe enough to cause significant problems in your job or relationships, such as losing a position or causing serious conflict, or have you generally been able to maintain your employment and social standing despite these struggles?\n\nPatient: I'm not entirely sure, and I haven't really thought about how much it has impacted my job stability or relationships specifically. It definitely makes things difficult in the moment, but I can't say for certain if it has caused major problems like losing a job or serious conflicts.\n\nDoctor: To clarify, do you experience these long, seven-month periods of high energy and irritability consistently, or are there times within those months where your mood settles down to a more neutral level?\n\nPatient: I'm not quite sure, and I haven't really thought about whether there are actually neutral periods in between. It mostly just feels like a continuous blur of high energy and irritability the entire time.\n\nDoctor: Could you tell me if you have ever had a distinct period of at least four consecutive days where your mood was elevated or irritable, which was clearly different from your usual self and noticeable to others?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have definitely experienced that, but it lasted much longer than four days—it went on for about seven months. During that time, my mood was abnormally high, euphoric, and full of energy, but I was also extremely irritable and would get angry over minor things. It was definitely different from my normal self, and those around me noticed the change immediately.\n\nDoctor: During these seven-month periods, did you ever engage in risky behaviors—such as excessive spending, reckless driving, or impulsive sexual encounters—that had serious, lasting consequences for your personal or financial life?\n\nPatient: Yes, during those seven months, I definitely experienced a significantly increased interest and activity when it came to sex. However, I don't recall engaging in other risky behaviors like excessive spending or reckless driving."
